Now, maybe there is a bug if after starting, we encounter a digit with `x < change[x]` and set `num[i]`, but if `x < change[x]` and `on` is True, we don't break. Good.

Now, maybe there is a bug if after starting, we encounter a digit with `x == change[x]` and then a digit with `x > change[x]`; code continues through equal and breaks at decreasing. Good.

Now, maybe there is a bug if before starting, we encounter a digit with `x == change[x]` and then a digit with `x < change[x]`; code skips equal and starts at beneficial. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the code's use of `enumerate(num)` after `num` is a list. If we modify `num[i]` to a string of length 1, the list length remains same. Good.

Now, maybe there is a bug if `change[x]` is greater than x but `change[x]` is a multi-digit number like 10? Constraint no. Good.

Now, maybe there is a bug if `change` is a tuple? No, list.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the "undefined_method" call. It is after loop, before return. Removing it is necessary. Good.

Now, perhaps the original code's `if x < change[x+1]` was intended to be `if x < change[x]` but also the `elif x > change[x] and on: break` should be `elif x > change[x] and on: break`; no issue.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the code's type annotation if `List` not imported. We'll include import. Good.

Now, maybe there is a bug with the method name "maximumNumber" vs "maximumNumber"? Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "num = list(num)" and then `for i, ch in enumerate(num)` while `num` is a list of characters. If we modify `num[i]`, the `ch` for current iteration is the original char. Good.

Now, maybe there is a bug if `change[x]` is less than x but `on` is True, break. But what if the current digit is at position i and there are previous digits that were mutated. If we break, we stop mutating. Good.

Now, maybe there is a bug if `change[x]` is less than x but `on` is False, skip. But what if there is a previous digit with change equal and later beneficial? Since on false, equal skipped. Good.

Now, maybe there is a bug if `change[x]` is less than x but `on` is False and later beneficial after another decreasing? We skip all decreasing/equal until first beneficial. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the "maximumNumber" method name. Good.

Now, perhaps the original code's `if x < change[x+1]` could be a typo for `if x < change[x]` but also the `elif x > change[x] and on: break` should be `elif x > change[x] and on: break` yes. But what if `x < change[x]` and on is true, we set. Good.

Now, maybe we should rename `on` to `changed` for clarity? Not necessary. But if we output fixed code, minimal changes are better. We can keep `on`. Good.

Now, maybe we should remove unused `ch`? Not necessary.

Now, final code with import:
```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maximumNumber(self, num: str, change: List[int]) -> str:
        num = list(num)
        on = False
        for i, ch in enumerate(num):
            x = int(ch)
            if x < change[x]:
                on = True
                num[i] = str(change[x])
            elif x > change[x] and on:
                break
        return "".join(num)
```
This is the answer.

But wait, let's consider if `change[x]` could be equal to x but we still need to set `on`? No, equal before start not beneficial. Equal after start no need. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a scenario where `change[x]` > x but x is 0 and change[0] = 9. Code sets. Good.

Now, maybe there is a bug with `num = list(num)` and then `for i, ch in enumerate(num)` while `num` is list of chars. If we modify `num[i]`, the `ch` for current iteration is the original char. Good.

Now, maybe there is a bug with `self.undefined_method()`: If we remove, no issue. Good.

Now, let's consider if the code should use `change[int(ch)]` instead of `change[x]`? Same.

Now, maybe there is a bug with `change[x+1]` if x=9, but if we fix to `change[x]`, no index error. Good.

Now, let's think about "repair-and-recheck" maybe they want us to identify all bugs: 
1. `change[x+1]` should be `change[x]`.
2. `self.undefined_method()` should be removed.
3. Maybe `elif x > change[x] and on: break` should be `elif x > change[x] and on: break`? No.
4. Maybe `num[i] = str(change[x])` should be `num[i] = str(change[x])`? Yes.

But wait, could there be a bug with the condition `if x < change[x+1]` and `elif x > change[x] and on`? Suppose `change[x]` is less than x but `change[x+1]` is greater than x. Original would mutate incorrectly. We fix.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with the `on` flag being set to True when `x < change[x]`. But what if `x < change[x]` and we are already on? It sets True again. Fine.

Now, maybe there is a bug if `change[x]` > x but `on` is False and we set `num[i] = str(change[x])`, but if `change[x]` is a multi-digit number? change values are digits 0-9, so single digit. Good.

Now, maybe there is a bug if `num` is a string of length 0, but constraints no.

Now, let's consider if the problem requires mutating exactly one substring, possibly empty? The standard solution returns original if no beneficial. If no beneficial, code returns original. Good.

Now, maybe there is a bug if there is a beneficial digit after a decreasing digit but before the first beneficial? Wait "first beneficial" is by definition first digit where change[d] > d. Any digit before it has change[d] <= d. If there is a decreasing digit before first beneficial, skip. If there is an equal digit before first beneficial, skip. Good.

Now, maybe there is a bug if there is a digit with change[d] > d, but later a digit with change[d] < d, and then later another digit with change[d] > d. The code breaks at the decreasing digit, so later beneficial not mutated. Is that always optimal? Yes because including decreasing digit would lower at that position, and any later increase cannot compensate. But what if you could choose substring that starts after the decreasing digit, skipping the earlier beneficial? That would miss earlier beneficial, which is worse. What if you could choose substring that includes the earlier beneficial and the later beneficial but not the decreasing digit? Not contiguous, impossible. So break is optimal. Good.

Now, maybe there is a bug if there is a digit with change[d] == d after start, and then a decreasing digit. Code continues through equal, then breaks at decreasing. Including equal doesn't hurt. Good.

Now, maybe there is a bug if there is a digit with change[d] == d before start, and then a decreasing digit, and then a beneficial digit. Code skips equal and decreasing, starts at beneficial. Good. Could there be a better substring starting at the equal digit? Equal no effect, so no. Starting at beneficial is same or better. Good.

Now, maybe there is a bug if there is a digit with change[d] < d before start, but the digit itself is less significant than later beneficial? Since before start, skipping is fine. Starting later is allowed. Good.

Now, let's think about if the code's `elif x > change[x] and on: break` should be `elif x > change[x] and on: break` but what if `x > change[x]` and on is True, but `change[x]` is equal to some previous digit? Break. Good.

Now, maybe there is a bug with `on` flag if `x < change[x]` and `on` is False, we set `on = True`. But if `x < change[x]` and `on` is True, we don't need to set but okay. Good.

Now, perhaps the original code's `if x < change[x+1]` was intended to be `if x < change[x]` but also the `elif x > change[x] and on: break` should be `elif x > change[x] and on: break`; no issue.

Now, let's consider if we should include `from typing import List` in final code. The prompt says "faulty python code" and includes `List[int]`. Now, final code with import:
```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maximumNumber(self, num: str, change: List[int]) -> str:
        num = list(num)
        on = False
        for i, ch in enumerate(num):
            x = int(ch)
            if x < change[x]:
                on = True
                num[i] = str(change[x])
            elif x > change[x] and on:
                break
        return "".join(num)
```

class Solution:
    def maximumNumber(self, num: str, change: List[int]) -> str:

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maximumNumber(self, num: str, change: List[int]) -> str:
        num = list(num)
        on = False
        for i, ch in enumerate(num):
            x = int(ch)
            if x < change[x]:
                on = True
                num[i] = str(change[x])
            elif x > change[x] and on:
                break
        return "".join(num)
